## Chapter 8 — DEEP DIVE — The Night in Florence

*2011 to 2013. AlexNet, built part by part in Hinton's Toronto lab: a photo as a grid of lights, one stencil and its worked number (a convolution), stencils on stencils, stencils learned from noise, a weir that keeps strong signals strong, too few labels for sixty million knobs (crops, mirrors, and dropout, so no lantern can count on another), two halves that split the work their own way, and five days downhill. Then Florence, 12 October 2012, without its professor: 15.3% against 26.2%, a cliff, then the fair caveats. Two candles go out (speech had gone first; within a year every top team used such nets), one is lit and gets its name, deep learning, a desk lamp at the rim clicks on after thirty-six years, and a prophecy goes on the Wall.*

Under it, the two steady hands bring a tall candle into the lamp's pool; on 'belief' (as the narration states the belief and its date aloud) they set it on the candle shelf beneath the board, into its year's place (left of the 2006 warm-up candle; CANDLE PLACEMENT), lettered (paraphrase, small caps, Plex Mono) TO SEE, A MACHINE NEEDS FEATURES DESIGNED BY EXPERTS; its base tag reads 'lit 1999'. CANDLE-CLOCK RULE (canonical; repeated word for word in every candle shot): every belief candle is cut to the same lit height, 45 scored rings at a pitch of 6 px per ring at L3 (270 px), its flame on the top ring and a small tag at its base giving the year it was lit. It burns down one ring per year the belief is held. The wax that burns away is never lost: it stays above the stub as a ghost paper sleeve, ring-scored and translucent in the haze, so sleeve plus stub is always the full lit height and the SLEEVE's length is the years (16 rings = 96 px; 39 rings = 234 px). The sleeves are the bars of the chart: a longer sleeve means a belief held longer. A candle still lit has a live flame where sleeve meets stub; one put out keeps its sleeve, dark, with a thread of smoke; one that guttered has a frayed rim. Here eleven rings have already burned (1999 to 2010): a ghost sleeve of eleven scored rings (66 px) stands above the stub, the flame live where sleeve meets stub. Finished stencils hang on hooks along the back wall like tools. ### ch08-s04 · a stitched tapestry of the digit 4, a pierced 3 × 3 tin lantern, and a wall that fills with spots of light · 2012 · candleAmber · in: crossfade

_THIS SHOT: a pierced 3 × 3 tin stencil over a stitched digit: nine holes weigh the light, the sum is inked, below zero goes dark, and the same stencil slides everywhere to fill a wall of bright spots. WORD RULE: the narration calls this object a tin stencil; 'lantern' is reserved film-wide for units (the lantern network), so no label or caption here calls the stencil a lantern (the kit name `stencilLantern` stays internal). Teaching machine `stencilLantern` (kernel 'vertical', the real Sobel-style weights −1 0 1 / −2 0 2 / −1 0 1), staged as a paper theatre: the stitched 14 × 14 tapestry of a handwritten '4' at left, the pierced tin stencil hanging over it, the plaster wall at right where its beam falls, ruled into the 12 × 12 feature map. Pierced holes are positive weights (sized by the weight), dark shutters with a cool rim are negative. On 'pierced' showKernel rises: the nine weights are inked beside the nine holes on the stencil itself (part of the machine, not a caption). On 'weighs' the camera pushes into the stencil; the nine weights glow one by one over the nine threads beneath. On 'Add' a parchment plate inks in the nine products, one per hole, middle column included (its weights are zero, so those three terms are visibly 0). On 'zero' a shuttered position over the stroke's right edge shows its sum, −4, rectified to 0: the wall cell stays dark. On 'edge' the stencil glides to the stroke's LEFT edge (row 10, column 7, step 127); the parchment plate shows the worked cell with all nine real values in row order (window rows 0.12 0.24 1 / 0 0.12 1 / 0 0.12 1, verified against DIGIT4 and convolve() in stencilLantern.ts): max(0, (−1)(0.12) + (0)(0.24) + (1)(1) + (−2)(0) + (0)(0.12) + (2)(1) + (−1)(0) + (0)(0.12) + (1)(1)) = 3.88; the beam flares and the brightest spot on the wall lights. On 'Slide' the stencil returns to step 0 and steps in raster order, on twos, accelerating, each wall cell lighting with its real rectified dot product until the full feature map glows: a bright vertical bar wherever a stroke's left edge is. On 'convolution' the camera eases back: the one stencil's shadow is seen repeated, faintly, at every position across the wall (the same nine weights everywhere). Hold on the finished wall in the music. An ILLUSTRATIVE tag sits small at frame bottom from 'pierced' on: this hand-set 3 × 3 stencil on a 14 × 14 digit is a teaching toy; AlexNet's real first-layer stencils are 96 learned 11 × 11 colour stencils (ch08-s05/s06). The general formula spot = max(0, Σ wᵢ · xᵢ) moves to the end-credits sources card; the narration says it in words. TEXT RULE: besides stamps and name cards, at most two text items in this shot. The kernel's numbers live on the stencil; the −4 → 0 shows only as the wall cell's own value._

> **[label]** ILLUSTRATIVE · a toy stencil, set by hand · AlexNet's first-layer stencils: 11 × 11 × 3, in colour, learned
> **[equation]** max(0, (−1)(0.12) + (0)(0.24) + (1)(1) + (−2)(0) + (0)(0.12) + (2)(1) + (−1)(0) + (0)(0.12) + (1)(1)) = 3.88
> **[term]** ReLU

**L0806** Hold a pierced tin stencil over the picture, a light behind it.  _(slow, demonstrative, like holding up a real object)_

**L0807** Each of its nine holes weighs the light beneath it. Add them up. Below zero counts as zero.  _(three distinct steps, a beat between each)_

**L0808** Over an edge, the sum is nearly four: a bright spot. Now the lovely part: slide the same stencil everywhere. A convolution.  _(the payoff; 'the sum is nearly four' plainly, the number landing on the bright spot; real delight on 'Now the lovely part'; let the slide fall in rhythm with the stencil's steps; then name the idea plainly, as a gift)_

### ch08-s05 · a cathedral of stencils rising block by block from a rose window that is the photograph · 2012 · dawnGold · in: crossfade

_THIS SHOT: a cathedral of stencils rises from the photograph's rose window, five courses of detectors built from detectors, then two great halls and a last hall of a thousand lanterns. Teaching machine `alexnetCathedral` (labels on). The husky photograph from s03 becomes the ROSE WINDOW at the base of a two-tower cathedral façade against a dawnGold sky. On 'ninety-six' the first course of stone rises: the conv1 blocks with face width 55 (true shape), and on the rose window's tracery 96 tiny 11 × 11 tiles flicker in (the first-layer stencils). On 'four' the second course rises at face width 27, then three courses at 13, 13, 13 (reveal driven block by block). Between courses, as each course face narrows (55 to 27, 27 to 13), a tiny wordless 3 × 3 window passes over the lower course's bright spots and keeps only the brightest cell of each window (overlapping max-pooling, 3 × 3, stride 2: a diagram mark, no caption), so the eye sees why the picture shrinks, each block's windows lighting as the flow climbs; on 'stencilling' the bright spots of one course are seen feeding up as the picture the next course's stencils slide over (detectors built from detectors). The layer vignettes appear ONE AT A TIME, in the rose-window position itself (never as simultaneous insets): as each course lights, the rose window briefly shows what such a layer responds to, then returns to the photograph: on 'edges' oriented strokes; on 'textures' woven textures; on 'parts' a fur patch and an eye; on 'things' a whole dog's head. On 'halls' TWO great halls rise above the five courses (fc6 and fc7, 4,096 lanterns each; in each, threads fan from every lantern to every lantern below, 'every lantern sees everything'), and on 'last' a third, last hall rises above them that IS a band of a thousand small lanterns, one per category, each with a tiny category tag (fc8, the output; nothing stacks above it; WORD RULE: 'door' is reserved for the chat box in ch12, so this band is never drawn as doors), as the camera pulls back to see the whole cathedral. (Five stencil courses plus three fully connected stages, two great halls and the hall of a thousand lanterns: the paper's five convolutional and three fully connected layers. ### ch08-s07 · a parchment plate: a weir across a stream, and two paper boats racing · 2012 · dawnGold · in: paperWipe

_THIS SHOT: a weir across a stream: the S-curve squashes a tall wave flat, the weir lets it pass at full height, and the rectifier's paper boat wins the race. LEFT: a stream crossing a small weir; it is the same below-zero rule from the lantern shot, now named as a picture. Water levels arriving from the left are signed numbers; everything below the lip (negative) stops dead, everything above flows over unchanged: the rectifier, f(x) = max(0, x), plotted faintly beneath as its kinked line. On 'squashed' a faint ghost curve of the older S-shaped switch (tanh) inks in beside it and flattens a tall wave against its ceiling; on 'undimmed' the same tall wave passes the weir at full height (ReLU does not saturate for large positive inputs); on 'tug' the backward bead from ch05 runs back across the weir and its ring of light stays bright, where across the S-curve it would have faded. On 'six' a small race: two paper boats, the rectifier's crossing a finish mark well before the old switch's (in their four-layer test on small pictures, not AlexNet itself). Its one label (the rectifier and its measured speed-up) sets in with the weir and is read under the rest of the line. (Max-pooling is not narrated; it is shown wordlessly between the cathedral's courses in ch08-s05.) TEXT RULE: besides stamps and name cards, at most two text items in this shot. ### ch08-s13b · the study's candle shelf beneath the case board; the oxblood question-card thread · 2012 · candleAmber · in: crossfade

_THIS SHOT: two candles go out (hand-cut stencils at thirteen rings, fiddly nets after ten to fifteen), the fog question is answered and unpinned, and LEARNED FEATURES is lit as the light finally finds the tall man. CAMERA: SHELF (low macro along the three candles), a tilt up to BOARD (the Q3 unpin as an insert), down to SHELF again, then the flame push onto the stage. Reverse FLAME PUSH, never through black (about 1.2 s, crossfadeMood dawnGold to candleAmber, STUDY LOCK): the workshop's last slanting light narrows to a single small flame, and we pull back out of it to find it is the expert-features candle's flame on the shelf. The long paper-cut candle shelf beneath the case board. CANDLE-CLOCK RULE (canonical; repeated word for word in every candle shot): every belief candle is cut to the same lit height, 45 scored rings at a pitch of 6 px per ring at L3 (270 px), its flame on the top ring and a small tag at its base giving the year it was lit. It burns down one ring per year the belief is held. The wax that burns away is never lost: it stays above the stub as a ghost paper sleeve, ring-scored and translucent in the haze, so sleeve plus stub is always the full lit height and the SLEEVE's length is the years (16 rings = 96 px; 39 rings = 234 px). The sleeves are the bars of the chart: a longer sleeve means a belief held longer. A candle still lit has a live flame where sleeve meets stub; one put out keeps its sleeve, dark, with a thread of smoke; one that guttered has a frayed rim. Three candles matter here. The expert-features candle (TO SEE, A MACHINE NEEDS FEATURES DESIGNED BY EXPERTS, tag 'lit 1999'): on 'thirteen' it gutters and goes out with thirteen rings burned (1999 to 2012), a dark ghost sleeve of thirteen scored rings (78 px) above its stub, a thread of smoke rising; its tag is re-inked '1999 – 2012 · 13'. On 'next' a brief ghost of the 2013 leaderboard of lanterns forms in that smoke, its top five lanterns all small cathedrals (no caption), and thins away: the flip took one year. Further left, the great 1990s candle from ch06 (NEURAL NETS ARE FIDDLY, AND BEATEN ANYWAY), still burning, its ghost sleeve fifteen rings long (90 px), the top five rings FRAYED and thinning (the bible's 'roughly 10–15 years depending on subfield': when the belief took hold, and when each field let go, is a matter of degree, speech before vision): on 'fiddly-nets' its flame bends and goes out; its tag is inked '– 2012 · 10–15 years, by field'. Just before, as 'Beside it' begins, a small paper tag, hung by a thread from the fiddly-nets candle's holder, turns into the light (world-space, so it is seen again whenever the shelf is in frame): 'speech first: Android voice search, July 2012' (bible Era 7: deep learning's first mass-market win came in speech, about three months before AlexNet). The two dark sleeves now stand side by side as two bars. On 'choose' the camera tilts up to the case board. BOARD MAP (canonical, from the staging appendix; repeated word for word): the Case Board is a fixed grid of slots laid out as network columns by era, left to right: C1 (1943–1969) the far-left input column, C2 (1969–1997), C3 (2006–2017), C4 (2018–2023), and the output, the glowing chat box, at the far right; the case card WHO TAUGHT IT? hangs in slot K at the board's centre; the suspect cards S1 RULES, S2 KNOWLEDGE and S3 LEARNING hang in the left margin, top to bottom; the founding row F1–F2 runs beneath columns C3 and C4; the question-card thread runs along the board's top edge (slots Q1 to Q4, left to right), a low-chroma oxblood string (emberRed rim mixed about 60% into candleAmber) that glints only where the lamp's pool falls; the Wall of Forecasts hangs to the board's right, its slots W1 to W8 left to right (W1 Turing, W2 chess, W3 Rosenblatt 1961, W4 Florence 2012, W5 Coulom, W6 the 30-to-50-years slip in shadow, W7 Hinton 2017, W8 an empty pin until ch15). The hands unpin the oxblood-thread card 'Why would anyone choose the fog?' from its fixed slot on the oxblood thread (the 1990s position) and it drops into the lamp's pool. On 'bowl' the camera follows it down: it lands at the foot of the dark expert-features sleeve, beside a small hand-cut tin stencil (the clean bowl stood on hand-cut stencils). On 'data' a NEW candle beside the two dark sleeves is lit, its lettering painted on the wax as part of the prop (no separate caption): LEARNED FEATURES, GIVEN DATA AND ARITHMETIC, tag '2012', no sleeve yet, its flame on the top ring (still burning, as of October 2026); on 'own' a faint learned-filter tile (a grey striped edge) glints in its wax. On 'data', with the new candle, the Computer History Museum card (its 'before' sentence only, verbatim; the newly lit candle beside it is the 'after') rises beside the new candle and stays up to the end of the shot. Just after the line, on the 'push' beat, once the CHM card has set in: a FLAME PUSH, never through black (about 1.2 s, crossfadeMood candleAmber → dawnGold): the camera pushes INTO the new candle's flame and out onto its own CANDLE STAGE (the paper stage of ch04-s08, now warm), the CHM card held at screen level through the push. On the 'desk' beat the camera WIDENS on that stage, keeping the new candle and the CHM card in frame, to reveal the far rim of its pool, where the plain wooden desk from ch04-s08 stands, empty, its reading lamp dark since 1976. L0832b is spoken over the dark desk ('Thirty-six years at the edge of the light'), and on 'Now' (the 'desklamp' beat; the line ends 'Now, it found him') the new candle's warm pool widens just enough to reach it and the desk's little reading lamp clicks on (no figure shown; the tall man is never seated in this era): the thirty-six-year promise of L0416, paid off in words and picture. ## Chapter 9 — The Gold Rush

*2012–2017. The 2012 candle becomes a creed: deep learning works on everything. By one reporter's account, a man who will not sit auctions a company of three to Google; DeepMind's Go machine, which learned from people, then from itself, arrives about eight years early, and Lee Sedol answers it once; months before Seoul, OpenAI is founded on hope and fear. At the rim, doubters, and the candle burns on; then a slip goes up on the Wall, and nobody says a word.*

### ch09-s01 · Lake Tahoe, Nevada, December 2012, late night: a hotel room (Room 731), a laptop propped on an upturned wastebasket · 2010s · serverCyan · in: cut

_THIS SHOT: a hotel room at Lake Tahoe: a new candle's pool spreads across the floor as sealed bids slide along a thread and the tall man stops the auction. CHAPTER INDEX TAB (the film's chapter-card device for clue chapters): at the shot's first frame a manila index tab, typed in Special Elite with the chapter title 'The Gold Rush', drops down from the frame's TOP edge at top-left, like a file-divider tab (the manila tab's own corner, clear of the bottom-left date and place stamps), holds for 2.5 seconds over the picture, and slides back up; no new shot, no lamp change. CAMERA: the hotel room (era location; no study visit), the creed candle on its stage in the out-of-focus foreground. Hard match cut (same serverCyan mood as chapter 8's last shot, so no lamp change): on the blink of chapter 8's green status LED, its glow becomes the cool screen of a laptop in a dark hotel room. Wide, static at first. Mid-plane: a tall man in silhouette (Hinton, 2000s–2020s puppet, blazer, swept-back hair) STANDS, straight-backed, at a laptop balanced on an upturned wastebasket, the only way he can type standing. Behind him a window of black pines and a band of snow-light; a hotel bed he never sits on, cut as one flat dark shape. On 'account' a small label, 'as reported by Cade Metz', sets in at lower left and stays for the whole shot: everything in this room is one reporter's account. Foreground, out of focus: the new candle from chapter 8, LEARNED FEATURES, GIVEN DATA AND ARITHMETIC, now huge on a paper stage (lit in 2012, its ghost sleeve not yet one ring tall), and its pool of light falls exactly where he stands. CANDLE-CLOCK RULE (canonical; repeated word for word in every candle shot): every belief candle is cut to the same lit height, 45 scored rings at a pitch of 6 px per ring at L3 (270 px), its flame on the top ring and a small tag at its base giving the year it was lit. It burns down one ring per year the belief is held. The wax that burns away is never lost: it stays above the stub as a ghost paper sleeve, ring-scored and translucent in the haze, so sleeve plus stub is always the full lit height and the SLEEVE's length is the years (16 rings = 96 px; 39 rings = 234 px). The sleeves are the bars of the chart: a longer sleeve means a belief held longer. A candle still lit has a live flame where sleeve meets stub; one put out keeps its sleeve, dark, with a thread of smoke; one that guttered has a frayed rim. Slow push-in (about 6% over the shot). On 'tall' the pool brightens around his feet. On 'three-person' a small tilt down shows the laptop on the upturned wastebasket (the narration names neither; the picture carries both). On 'auctioned', four sealed paper envelopes slide in along a thread from the dark at frame right, each a bid; their wax seals glow and swell with each round. Just after 'company' one envelope (the earliest bidder) drops off the thread; a second drops soon after; the bids climb on a typewritten tally that ticks up in the screen's glow. On 'forty-four' his hand lifts off the keys, palm down, and the tally freezes at 44,000,000. On 'Google', the line's last word (beat choseGoogle), his hand draws one of the two remaining envelopes off the thread and lays it face UP beside the laptop, its typed address a single large word, GOOGLE, caught in the screen's glow (prop lettering on the envelope, not a label); the last envelope drifts back into the dark (the $44M lives on the frozen tally, a prop; TEXT RULE: 'as reported by Cade Metz' is the shot's only label besides the place stamp and the chapter index tab; the deal's announcement, March 2013, is in the published sources list). Ear and eye now both know his new employer, which chapter 13's 'He left Google' depends on. Hold on the still figure in the candle's pool. No logos anywhere; the bidders are blank envelopes, and Google appears only as the typed word on the chosen envelope._

Marcus, 2018', not a label; only its first clause is typed large, the rest of the sentence small and illegible, saved for ch15-s09). Nobody speaks after the doubters' line: the narrator deliberately says nothing about the slip. A single draught moves the slip's corner once, late in the hold. This is the plant for the film's last spoken quotation (chapter 15). TEXT RULE: besides stamps and name cards, at most one text item in this shot (the Gender Shades label); the panda captions are typed on the prop. ROUND 5 (runtime): the carried slip is NOT captioned here; its typed lettering stays small and only half-caught by the lamp, a plant like the W6 slip in shadow. It is read aloud and shown in full, with its attribution, in ch15-s09._

> **[label]** gender misread: darker-skinned women up to 34.7% · lighter-skinned men up to 0.8% · Buolamwini & Gebru, 2018

**L0910** At the rim, doubters. Changes too small to see could fool these nets. Joy Buolamwini and Timnit Gebru found commercial face software misread the gender of darker-skinned women far more often than of lighter-skinned men. The candle burned on, and still does, argued over.  _(fair and steady, the voice of a careful witness; no alarm, no scolding; 'too small to see' with quiet wonder; the two names unhurried and clear; the face finding given its full, plain weight; 'The candle burned on, and still does, argued over.' level and quiet, neither triumph nor warning; then silence for the slip)_

♪ The pulse drops away entirely. Under L0910 a thin low drone only, with one dry muted tick on 'fool' and soft ticks under the falling stamp; on 'candle burned on' the candle motif's rising fourth, very soft, unresolved (the creed still lit); nothing ominous, nothing resolving. Then solo celesta states the slip motif, which must be clear enough to be recognised when it returns in chapter 15: the candle motif's first two notes only (rising fourth, then the held suspension), exposed, as the slip is pinned; it stops there, unresolved. After a breath it states the same two notes once more, softer, on the draught. Otherwise near-silence, a faint room tone, until the cut. Chapter 15 resolves it.

## Chapter 10 — DEEP DIVE — Everything Looks at Everything

*2014–2018. The Transformer, built one part at a time. For twenty years language had to be read in order; then attention, and a lunch-table idea that colleagues doubted. Fire the slow reader, keep the attention: three cards per word, one jug of light, a division by eight, eight heads, a clock face for position, talk then think six times. The title, why it won and what it costs, a twenty-year candle put out, and the writing half of the machine, with its curtain over the words to come.*

### ch10-s01 · a long, high hall of hanging paper word-slips at dusk, out of time (the inside of a recurrent network); the candle shelf at its foot · 2010s · duskRose · in: fadeBlack

_THIS SHOT: a long hall of hanging word-slips where a scribe carries one lamp from slip to slip in order, and the light he carries fades to a glow-worm at the far end. CHAPTER INDEX TAB (the film's chapter-card device for clue chapters): at the shot's first frame a manila index tab, typed in Special Elite with the chapter title 'DEEP DIVE · Everything Looks at Everything', drops down from the frame's top edge at top-left, like a file-divider tab on a manila folder, holds for 2.5 seconds over the picture, and slides back up; no new shot, no lamp change. CAMERA: the long hall (era location), with one SHELF ledge slid into its foreground (low macro on the read-in-order candle). From black, a single lamp, under the tab. Silently, in the lead-in (no line names it), a ghost of chapter 8's pierced stencil hangs in the haze above the hall and thins away into it (convolution is not in the Transformer; only its lesson, learn the features, carries on), before the scribe sets off. We are at one end of a very long hall: a line of paper word-slips hangs from a beam, receding into haze, dozens of them. A small cut-paper scribe (no real person) walks the line with one lamp, stopping at every slip in turn: he reads it, then passes the lamp's flame forward to a wick on the next slip, step by step, on twos. The camera tracks with him slowly, then pulls back to show how long the line is. This is chapter 6's whisper made visible: the light he carries dims a little at each hand-off, so by the far end it is a glow-worm (the vanishing signal; at about slip 50 it is nearly gone). On 'candle' a short ledge of the candle shelf slides into the hall's foreground and its candle is picked out, standing in its year's place on the shelf (CANDLE PLACEMENT: by the year on the base tag, so left of the 1999 and 2006 candles) (STUDY LOCK: the candle's own flame throws a small candleAmber pool, a practical inside the duskRose hall; the study shelf proper is seen in candleAmber in ch10-s13b): LANGUAGE MUST BE READ IN ORDER (lettered on the candle itself in small caps; base tag 1997). CANDLE-CLOCK RULE (canonical; repeated word for word in every candle shot): every belief candle is cut to the same lit height, 45 scored rings at a pitch of 6 px per ring at L3 (270 px), its flame on the top ring and a small tag at its base giving the year it was lit. It burns down one ring per year the belief is held. The wax that burns away is never lost: it stays above the stub as a ghost paper sleeve, ring-scored and translucent in the haze, so sleeve plus stub is always the full lit height and the SLEEVE's length is the years (16 rings = 96 px; 39 rings = 234 px). The sleeves are the bars of the chart: a longer sleeve means a belief held longer. A candle still lit has a live flame where sleeve meets stub; one put out keeps its sleeve, dark, with a thread of smoke; one that guttered has a frayed rim. Here the candle is still lit: a ghost sleeve about seventeen rings tall (102 px) stands above the stub, the flame where sleeve meets stub; it will go out at twenty rings in ch10-s13b. At its foot, small, the chapter 6 'protected pipe' (the long-memory unit) glows on 'pipe', the callback the line makes: the date the candle was lit, and the reason the belief held so long. In the short music-only hold after L1002 the glow-worm light crawls on down the long dim line (Polosukhin's 'a thousand cycles' quote moves to the end-credits sources card)._

> **[title]** DEEP DIVE · Everything Looks at Everything

**L1002** Another candle, lit in 1997 with that protected pipe: language must be read in order.  _(a breath after chapter 9; a small nod back on 'that protected pipe'; then slow and rhythmic, each comma a step of the scribe)_

♪ Opens the deep dive in duskRose: solo cello with a slow, metronomic plucked step locked to the scribe's hand-offs (on twos), intensity 0.3. The candle motif's rising fourth on the lit candle. Each step a touch quieter, so the music itself fades down the line.

> **[equation]** 2 · 0 · 4 · 2  ÷ √4  →  1 · 0 · 2 · 1

## Chapter 11 — Read Everything

*2018–2022. Pre-training comes back in a different coat, and the attention threads light up inside the chat box. The 1985 card from the file meets a one-sentence training goal, and the typesetter's case answers the prologue's riddle of the missing rulebook. A smooth law on log paper becomes a city of a hundred and seventy-five billion weights that can learn a task from a few examples, and a new candle is lit: scale is the main lever, and the rim of its light grows crowded.*

## Chapter 12 — The Last Accomplice, and the Return to the Scene

*2017–2023. The last accomplice is a person, choosing: a simulated robot learns from a person's choices between two tries, and people's rankings train a judge that shapes a small model until it is preferred to one a hundred times larger. Then the return to the dark room: not a new engine, a new door, and the first note in the file, written that same day, still holds. The X-ray is complete and Rosenblatt's 1961 note is read over the glowing box: language was the door. A step back to 2021: the first figure through that door is a new lab, founded and told in the same grammar as every lab's founding, money and all (FTX, then Amazon and Google on the ledger). Then a stampede through the door, in the order it happened, ending with OpenAI's GPT-4 and Anthropic's Claude launched the same day; and on the Wall, one slip still half in shadow.*

### ch12-s02 · A paper-cut garden at dusk, 2022: a long overgrown hedge, a small neat topiary and a pair of shears; a labeller's desk with two scrolls · 2022 · duskRose · in: crossfade

### ch12-s03 · The case board in the study, a step back to 2021 (founded in January; the announcement pinned is 28 May), under the green-shaded lamp; a ledger on the side table · 2021 · candleAmber · in: cut

_THIS SHOT: the founding grammar again, a step back to 2021: Anthropic's announcement pinned bare in the founding row, the same warm and cold hand-lanterns on one nail, and the same plain ledger page for the money. CAMERA: BOARD. A plain cut along the study (STUDY LOCK, study to study, no lamp change) from the full wide of ch12-s07 to the founding row of the case board, for a step back in time: the first figure through the new door. The step back is carried by the date stamp and the narration ('a lab born in 2021'). FOUNDING GRAMMAR (canonical; the same objects, word for word, for every lab's founding, OpenAI's in ch09-s04 and Anthropic's here): a typewritten announcement pinned in the founding row; no name card under the pin (the founders are named only in the narration); a warm and a cold hand-lantern hanging from the same nail, whose meanings never change from lab to lab: the WARM lantern is always the promise the lab names, the COLD lantern is always the danger the lab names (OpenAI: warm on 'benefit', cold on 'damage'; Anthropic: cold on 'safety', the risk its name answers, warm on 'research'; the order of swings may differ, the meanings may not); and one plain ledger page in IBM Plex Mono for the lab's money. This shot is staged exactly as OpenAI's founding in chapter 9, and must match it. The date stamp reads 'FOUNDED JAN 2021 · ANNOUNCED 28 MAY 2021', because Anthropic's founding (January 2021) and its public announcement (28 May 2021) are different days, whereas OpenAI's 11 December 2015 stamp is its founding announcement; the stamp must not let a bare announcement date read as the founding date. No office, no garage, no spotlight, no faces, no hero framing, flat even light. On 'founded' the hands pin a typewritten announcement dated 28 May 2021 into its slot on the case board (slot F2 of the founding row, beneath column C4: the same height and size as OpenAI's 2015 announcement in F1); its text stays faint except the one phrase the narrator speaks, 'an AI safety and research company', which lights on 'safety' (exactly as ch09-s04 lights only its spoken phrases; no separate quote card, so no dollar figure appears off the ledger). No name cards under the pin: the founders are named only in the narration, exactly as OpenAI's pin in ch09-s04 carries no names (both pins bare). On 'safety', the COLD hand-lantern swings into light on the pin (the danger the name answers, as OpenAI's cold lantern swung on 'damage'); on 'research' the WARM one swings beside it (the promise, as OpenAI's warm lantern swung on 'benefit'): the same pair, the same meanings, on one nail. The lanterns are unlabelled and carry no verdict. After the second line, in a silence of about 2.5 s with no narration (L1208 was cut in review round 9; the ledger carries the money alone), the ledger on the side table turns a page; in the same plain ledger style used for every lab's money in the film (OpenAI's in ch09-s04; Microsoft–OpenAI arrives in shot 8), the entries are set in IBM Plex Mono: '$500M · FTX (later exposed as fraud; Anthropic not implicated)', then, a beat later, 'billions · Amazon, Google'. Every dollar figure for Anthropic is on the ledger and nowhere else. Nothing comments on any entry. No logos. STUDY LOCK: the study is candleAmber, the same lamp as every other study beat; no era tint on the board, the ledger or the lanterns. L1206 opens 'Among the runners…'; the rewritten L1219b closes the shot: Bard, LLaMA, then GPT-4 and Claude chips set down on the desk as each is named._

## Chapter 13 — The Case Reopened

*2023. A pioneer takes down his own forecast, a new candle of worry is lit and the debate splits into three lamps, not two teams.*

### ch13-s01 · The detective's study at night: the Wall of Forecasts pinned beside the dark case board; then, through it, the paper stage under the candle of 2012 · May 2023 · 2020s · candleAmber · in: lampChange

_THIS SHOT: the case reopens: Hinton takes his own 30-to-50-years slip off the Wall, it darkens and curls in his hand, and the new question is asked aloud. CHAPTER INDEX TAB (the film's chapter-card device for clue chapters): at the shot's first frame a manila index tab, typed in Special Elite with the chapter title 'The Case Reopened', drops down from the frame's top edge at top-left like a file-divider tab, holds for 2.5 seconds over the picture, and slides back up; no new shot, no lamp change. CAMERA: WALL (a slow lateral truck to W6), then the push through the slip onto the stage. (The truck, the settle on the slip and the push through it all play wordless in the lead-in, under the tab; the first line begins on the stage.) STUDY LOCK (staging appendix): every study, board, shelf and Wall beat is candleAmber, so the shot opens candleAmber and its mood field names the opening location only. Open on the Wall of Forecasts in the green lamp's pool, the camera trucking slowly right along its fixed slot order (see the staging appendix): Turing's curled 'about fifty years', the yellowed chess slip's empty pin, Rosenblatt's 1961 slip, Florence's 2012 slip, Coulom's glinting Go slip. The truck slows on the next slot, one slip half in shadow, the one whose half-lit corner we glimpsed in ch01-s05, ch06-s07 and ch09-s05: '30 TO 50 YEARS OR EVEN LONGER' in typewriter type, with no date of pinning shown. Just beyond it, at the far end, Hinton's 2017 'graduate student' slip sits in the dark, unvisited. The camera does not stop at the wall; it pushes through the slip's paper grain (the FLAME PUSH device from the staging appendix, anchored on the slip instead of a flame: a 1.2 s camera push to lens 1.6 with bloom up and crossfadeMood candleAmber to duskRose over the same 1.2 s, no foreground dip; never TRANSITIONS.lampChange inside a shot. The shot itself arrives from ch12 on a lampChange, for the chapter break) onto the paper stage of the still-burning 2012 candle LEARNED FEATURES, GIVEN DATA AND ARITHMETIC, where the same wall now stands huge as a backdrop. CANDLE-CLOCK RULE (canonical; repeated word for word in every candle shot): every belief candle is cut to the same lit height, 45 scored rings at a pitch of 6 px per ring at L3 (270 px), its flame on the top ring and a small tag at its base giving the year it was lit. It burns down one ring per year the belief is held. The wax that burns away is never lost: it stays above the stub as a ghost paper sleeve, ring-scored and translucent in the haze, so sleeve plus stub is always the full lit height and the SLEEVE's length is the years (16 rings = 96 px; 39 rings = 234 px). The sleeves are the bars of the chart: a longer sleeve means a belief held longer. A candle still lit has a live flame where sleeve meets stub; one put out keeps its sleeve, dark, with a thread of smoke; one that guttered has a frayed rim. In the centre of the candle's pool of light stands the tall man: lean, white-grey hair swept back, standing (as he has been since the mid-2000s), facing the wall. He reaches up with his near hand (pinch), lifts the slip off its pin in one slow arc on twos, and lowers it to look at it. The pin is left bare and catches a tiny glint; the slot stays empty for the rest of the film. Music drops out on 'down' and stays silent through the pause: only the candle's flicker and the faint paper rustle. As the quote is read, the quote card rises beside him in EB Garamond with speaker and date. He does not turn to camera._

> **[nameCard]** Geoffrey Hinton

## Chapter 14 — Two Shores

*2017 to 2026. The US and China as dated beliefs on two equally lit shores: a plan, a fence of rules, a price tag, a struck match, and a dialogue with two names.*

### ch14-s01 · Two skylines facing each other across dark water at dawn; a wooden jetty runs out from the middle with a Go board on it · July 2017 · 2010s · dawnGold · in: crossfade

_THIS SHOT: through the study window, two equal shores at dawn; the 2017 plan's parchment page rises over the right shore, its 2030 ring glowing, naming no rival; one stone is set on the jetty's Go board. CHAPTER INDEX TAB (the film's chapter-card device for clue chapters): at the shot's first frame a manila index tab, typed in Special Elite with the chapter title 'Two Shores', drops down from the frame's top edge at TOP-left like a file-divider tab, holds for 2.5 seconds over the picture, and slides back up (top-left, so it never shares the bottom-left corner with the date stamp); no new shot, no lamp change. CAMERA: through the study WINDOW (the WINDOW RULE below); the shelf appears only as a reflection in the glass. The lead-in here is short, so the tab rides over the first line, as ch02-s01's does. WINDOW RULE (canonical for this chapter; repeated word for word in every ch14 shot): the strait is always seen THROUGH the study window. The window's frame and mullions sit at the edges of the frame as a faint L1 occluder (ink silhouettes with candleAmber rim hairlines only, never crossing the centre of the image), so every ch14 picture reads as looking out from the study. Wherever the candles, a match or the narrator's hands appear, they are a reflection on ONE plane only, L2, at about 35% alpha with a slight water ripple, so the hands read as a reflection in the glass, never as giants over the water. KIT DEPENDENCY (blocking for film-shots, see staging.kitWork): finish/reflection(layerFn, {alpha, flip:false, ripple}), an offscreen sub-render of the study shelf composited over the water. Staging rule for the whole chapter: two skylines of equal height, equal mass and identical warmth face each other across a band of dark water, left and right, mirror-balanced in the frame. They are generic paper-cut cities (towers, bridges, a few hills), with no landmarks, flags, emblems, dragons, eagles or red tint, and their small lit windows flicker on the same rhythm. Whatever the mood, both shores always take exactly the same palette. Haze lies on the water. From the centre of the near bank a long wooden jetty runs out into the water, and on its end sits a Go board on a low stand, lit by a lantern hung on a post, with a stone bowl on each side. There is no candle shelf on the jetty: CANDLE PLACEMENT (canonical for this chapter; repeated in every candle shot): the China-race candles are NOT on the jetty. They stand at the right end of the study's own candle shelf, the same shelf ch15-s08 pulls back across, and are seen only as a reflection in the study window glass that ch13-s06 opened onto the strait: the reflected shelf, its flames and the narrator's steady hands float over the water. The reflection keeps the study's candleAmber (STUDY LOCK: the study's colour enters this chapter only as that reflection), while the strait and shores beyond keep the shot's own mood. Open on the study window frame itself: dark mullions against the dawn beyond, the unlit room behind the camera. Push through it, FLAME PUSH-style but with no lamp change and no mood crossfade (a short camera push to lens 1.6 through the gap between two mullions, which slide out to the frame edges and stay there as the faint L1 occluder), arriving wide and still on the two shores at dawn; then a slow push in toward the jetty. On the first line a single parchment page rises from the right shore and hangs in the air above it: the plan, stamped with the date, its three steps marked 2020 · 2025 · 2030 as three small rings, the last one glowing. On 'no rival' the camera eases back to show the whole page: there is no name on it but its own, and the left shore simply goes on glowing. A hand from the right side sets one stone on the Go board; nothing answers yet. CANDLE-CLOCK RULE (canonical; repeated word for word in every candle shot): every belief candle is cut to the same lit height, 45 scored rings at a pitch of 6 px per ring at L3 (270 px), its flame on the top ring and a small tag at its base giving the year it was lit. It burns down one ring per year the belief is held. The wax that burns away is never lost: it stays above the stub as a ghost paper sleeve, ring-scored and translucent in the haze, so sleeve plus stub is always the full lit height and the SLEEVE's length is the years (16 rings = 96 px; 39 rings = 234 px). The sleeves are the bars of the chart: a longer sleeve means a belief held longer. A candle still lit has a live flame where sleeve meets stub; one put out keeps its sleeve, dark, with a thread of smoke; one that guttered has a frayed rim._

## Chapter 15 — Case Status: Open

*2024 to 5 October 2026. A phone at two in the morning and a Nobel prize for neural networks; what the machines do now; two labs under the same light, each with its hard facts; the last witnesses at the rim and the oldest ones leaving the room. Then the rewind plays forward, the three suspects get their verdict, the case board turns out to be a lantern network with one lantern dark, the shelf becomes a bar chart in wax, the last slip is read, and a hand from our side of the screen lights a new candle.*

### ch15-s01 · A plain hotel room in California, 8 October 2024, about 2 a.m. · 2020s · candleAmber · in: fadeBlack

_THIS SHOT: a phone lights in an empty hotel room at 2 a.m.; its glow becomes a Nobel medallion, the Hall of Mirrors turns at last toward Hopfield, a critic's card rises, and a second medallion folds a protein. CHAPTER INDEX TAB (the film's chapter-card device for clue chapters): at the shot's first frame a manila index tab, typed in Special Elite with the chapter title 'Case Status: Open', drops down from the frame's top-left edge like a manila file-divider tab, holds for 2.5 seconds over the picture, and slides back up (top-left, so it never shares the bottom-left corner with the date stamp); no new shot, no lamp change. The tab rides over the first line (the lead-in is short), and is gone before the date stamp types in on 'phone'. Fade up from black on a dark, plain hotel room in paper cut-out: a narrow bed edge, a bedside table, a sash window with the faint glow of a car park light behind thin net drapes. Nobody is shown; the room is empty of figures (a private moment, kept private). On the bedside table a mobile phone lies face down, then lights and buzzes, throwing a small cold rectangle of light on the ceiling; on its second buzz Geoffrey Hinton's name card fades in beside the phone (the phone is his). Slow push in toward the phone. On 'Nobel' the phone's glow warms and blooms into a gold medallion (props/symbolic medallion, motif blank, tone gold) that rises out of the light and turns once, edge-on to face-on; a paper ribbon unrolls beneath it carrying the physics citation, set in EB Garamond in quotation marks. On 'Hopfield' a match cut: the medallion's rim becomes the arch of a mirror in the Hall of Mirrors from chapter 5, where a single tiny silhouette of the physicist (hopfield) stands at a lectern among the hundreds of mirrored figures that once did not look; now, slowly and one by one, the mirrored figures turn toward him. Name card fades in for him. On 'downhill' a faint engraved hollow appears on the medallion's blank face and a tiny paper ball rolls into it and settles (the memory from chapter 5); on 'cools' the haze around the medallion cools to pale and warms back (the Boltzmann machine of ch05-s03's card, now named aloud: 'a net that settles as it cools'; the cooling is how it settles into a state, not how it learns, as in ch05-s03; it was co-invented, so no single figure is shown for it; the eye and the ear agree and 'Was this physics?' has its handle). Dame Wendy Hall's dissent (L1528, voiced: the question 'Was this physics?' and her own words 'a bit of a stretch', spoken as a quotation; her name and fuller words on the card): on 'cheered' the physics ribbon's lettering fades and her quote card sets in on its own, read under the rest of the line and the pause that follows, and a single mirrored figure at the rim of the hall stays turned away, the only one that does. TEXT DISCIPLINE: besides the chapter tab, the date stamp and the name cards, two text items only, one after the other, never together: the physics citation on its ribbon (from 'Nobel' to 'cheered') and Dame Wendy Hall's quote card (from 'cheered' until 'chemistry'; 'a bit of a stretch' is also spoken); the DeepMind name card counts as a name card. THE CHEMISTRY PRIZE (L1529, restored in round 8 for balance: both 2024 science prizes went to neural-network work, and DeepMind is otherwise unvoiced in the present-day chapters; the first addition to drop if runtime must be held): on 'chemistry' the Hall card fades and, in the next arch along the hall, a second gold medallion of the same size and gold turns face-on, no ribbon text; the name card for Demis Hassabis and John Jumper of DeepMind fades in; on 'proteins' a thin paper ribbon on its blank face curls and folds itself into a small knotted protein shape and settles (a network that predicts the shapes of proteins; no figure is shown for them). It burns down one ring per year the belief is held. The wax that burns away is never lost: it stays above the stub as a ghost paper sleeve, ring-scored and translucent in the haze, so sleeve plus stub is always the full lit height and the SLEEVE's length is the years (16 rings = 96 px; 39 rings = 234 px). The sleeves are the bars of the chart: a longer sleeve means a belief held longer. A candle still lit has a live flame where sleeve meets stub; one put out keeps its sleeve, dark, with a thread of smoke; one that guttered has a frayed rim. So the shelf reads as a bar chart in wax. SHELF ORDER (one list, strictly by the year each candle was lit, left to right, from the staging candle table; never by the chapter in which it was filmed): 1 THE BRAIN IS A LOGIC MACHINE (1943) 16 rings, out; 2 A MACHINE THAT LEARNS WILL SOON SEE, TALK AND THINK (1958) about 7, frayed; 3 MANY-LAYERED NETS ARE A STERILE EXTENSION (1969) 17, out; 4 INTELLIGENCE IS KNOWLEDGE, TYPED IN BY HAND (c. 1980) about 7, out; 5 CYC (1984) 39, the tallest dark bar; 6 NEURAL NETS ARE FIDDLY, AND BEATEN ANYWAY (mid-1990s) about 16, frayed; 7 LANGUAGE MUST BE READ IN ORDER (1997) 20, out; 8 TO SEE, A MACHINE NEEDS FEATURES DESIGNED BY EXPERTS (1999) 13, out; 9 DEEP NETS MUST BE WARMED UP LAYER BY LAYER (2006) about 5, frayed; 10 LEARNED FEATURES, GIVEN DATA AND ARITHMETIC (2012) STILL LIT, mid-shelf; 11 A GO MACHINE IS A DECADE AWAY (2014) 2, out; 12 SCALE IS THE MAIN LEVER (2020) STILL LIT, trimmed; 13 COMPUTE IS THE CHOKEPOINT (2022) STILL LIT, contested; 14 CHINA IS A YEAR OR TWO BEHIND (2022) about 2, out; 15 SMARTER-THAN-HUMAN AI MAY BE YEARS AWAY, AND DANGEROUS (2023) STILL LIT; 16 the blackened match (January 2025), lying flat; 17 THE GAP IS MONTHS, NOT YEARS (2025) STILL LIT, contested; then, at the very end by the window, the empty place where ch15-s09's new candle will stand. So the still-lit flames (10, 12, 13, 15, 17) stand scattered among the dark sleeves, from mid-shelf to the window, not gathered at one end; sleeves of every length, long and short among each other, no trend by decade; any marked contested keep that tag, exactly as ch14-s07 leaves them. On 'forty' a soft light returns to Cyc's dark 39-ring sleeve (234 px), the tallest bar on the shelf (the film's own record, said aloud in ch13 as 'thirty-nine years'); on 'two' the short GO MACHINE sleeve (2 rings); on 'day' the blackened match; on 'burn' the five still-lit flames light up together across the whole shelf, from LEARNED FEATURES mid-shelf to THE GAP by the window, their base tags reading 'as of October 2026' (prop lettering), their short sleeves still growing. On 'lantern' the camera drifts back up to the network: one lantern in the middle layer has never lit, and the last oxblood-thread question card is pinned above it, swaying slightly (its words are the words being spoken). Below the board, on the desk, the brass microscope from ch15-s03 stands under the lamp, its lens turned toward the dark lantern. On 'answers' the dark lantern is the only dark thing on the board, and the attribution card for the limit of today's microscopes rises beside the microscope and holds through the pause and the music-only hold (enough to read it). TEXT DISCIPLINE: two text items only (the fast-forward carries none): the question card (from 'lantern') and the microscope-limit quote (from 'answers'). SCRIPT NOTE (review round 1): new line L1516b 'And how far, how fast? That file stays open.' after L1516; the case file lies open on 'file'; the hold after it is 5 s._

**L1516** One lantern is still dark: how, exactly, it reaches its answers.  _(very quiet)_

**L1516b** And how far, how fast? That file stays open.  _(very quiet, the second question answered honestly; 'how far, how fast?' unhurried; 'That file stays open' plain and level, no ominous weight)_

### ch15-s09 · The detective's study at night, October 2026; then darkness, and the end of the candle shelf · 2026 · candleAmber · in: cut

_THIS SHOT: Hinton's 2017 slip is read under the lamp and stamped STATUS: OPEN; the lamp clicks off, and a match lights one new, unlettered candle at the end of the shelf. CAMERA: WALL (W7), then FILE (top-down on the case card for the stamp), then the dark, then SHELF (the new candle at the shelf's end). Close on the Wall of Forecasts. In slot W7, the slip pinned in chapter 9 (September 2017; Hassabis's newer slip hangs beyond it at W8, W6's bare pin between them) lifts off its pin by itself and floats down into the pool of the desk lamp, where the two hands catch it and hold it under the light; its typed words are legible as the narrator reads them. Wordless, early in the pause after the quotation, the hands lay it on the WHO TAUGHT IT? case card and stamp the card with a rubber stamp: STATUS: OPEN, in red ink; the stamp says what the cut line used to say. A beat, long enough to read the red stamp. The desk lamp clicks off. Darkness, with only one thing visible: the text cursor from the very first shot, blinking where the chat box was. Then, on 'new candles' the cursor's blink slows; on 'come from', a hand reaches in from the bottom edge of the frame, from the viewer's side, an open palm holding a match; it strikes, and the flare lights the end of the candle shelf, where a new tall candle stands with no lettering and no rings scored on it yet. The hand lights it. The flame settles. Hold on the single new flame in the dark (holdAfter) as the music holds its suspension; the film's last words are 'come from', and the picture says the rest. Cut to black on the flame. CANDLE-CLOCK RULE (canonical; repeated word for word in every candle shot): every belief candle is cut to the same lit height, 45 scored rings at a pitch of 6 px per ring at L3 (270 px), its flame on the top ring and a small tag at its base giving the year it was lit. It burns down one ring per year the belief is held. The wax that burns away is never lost: it stays above the stub as a ghost paper sleeve, ring-scored and translucent in the haze, so sleeve plus stub is always the full lit height and the SLEEVE's length is the years (16 rings = 96 px; 39 rings = 234 px). The sleeves are the bars of the chart: a longer sleeve means a belief held longer. A candle still lit has a live flame where sleeve meets stub; one put out keeps its sleeve, dark, with a thread of smoke; one that guttered has a frayed rim._
